### Page 1

美联储副主席杰斐逊表⽰，未来政策调整需要仔细审视数据趋势，作出判断可能需要更多
时间。这与纽约联储主席威廉姆斯协调发声，叠加监管副主席鲍曼同步附和，三位永久票
委集体释放鸽派信号，直接将 10 ⽉加息概率从 70% 砸⾄ 25% ，市场预期重⼼移向 12 ⽉。洛
根等鹰派坚持⾄少再加息 50 基点，但难撼⼤局。今天⾮农及沃什 10 ⽉ 16 ⽇ IMF 讲话成关键
变量。
摘要：
美联储内部权⼒核⼼连续发出 " 按兵不动 " 信号，市场对 10 ⽉加息的押注⼏近⽡解。
美联储副主席杰斐逊（ Philip Jefferson ）周四发表讲话，措辞与纽约联储主席威廉姆斯（ John
Williams ）本周早些时候的表态⾼度⼀致，两⼈均强调在决定下⼀步政策⾏动之前需要更多时间
评估数据。
分析认为，由于 “ ⼆号⾼管 ” 杰斐逊与 “ 三号⾼管 ” 威廉姆斯同属⽀持主席沃什的⾮正式核⼼领导⼩
组 “ 三驾⻢⻋ ” ，两⼈的协调发声被市场解读为美联储⾼层集体意志的体现，⽽⾮个别官员的⼀家
之⾔。受此影响，联邦基⾦期货市场对 10 ⽉ FOMC 会议加息的定价概率从本周初约 70% 骤降⾄

约 25% ，市场预期重⼼已明显转向 12 ⽉。
尽管达拉斯联储主席洛根（ Lorie Logan ）、明尼阿波利斯联储主席卡什卡利（ Neel Kashkari ）
等鹰派官员周四当⽇亦发表讲话，坚持认为通胀仍过⾼、需要进⼀步收紧，但其声⾳在杰斐逊
的鸽派表态⾯前明显失⾊。
与此同时，美国国债市场在欧洲债市动荡引发的避险买盘推动下， 10 年期美债收益率从 24 年⾼
位回落，进⼀步强化了市场对加息预期降温的判断。
“ 三驾⻢⻋ ” 协调发声，鸽派信号具权威性
杰斐逊周四在弗吉尼亚⼤学达顿商学院发表讲话时表⽰，“ 展望未来，我的观点是，未来任何政
策调整都应通过仔细审视数据趋势、不断演变的前景以及⻛险平衡来决定。我和我的同事们需
要做出⾃⼰的判断，⽽这可能需要更多时间。 ”
他同时指出，⾃ 9 ⽉会议以来，整个期限结构的收益率均有所上升，表明投资者正在重新评估宏
观经济形势。他还提到，能源价格上涨、 AI 投资热潮以及关税等多重冲击正在叠加影响经济。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

杰斐逊的表态与威廉姆斯周⼆在布法罗⼤学的讲话形成呼应。威廉姆斯当时明确表⽰，在美联
储 9 ⽉加息之后，调整当前货币政策⽴场 " 没有紧迫性 " ，同时表⽰今年晚些时候 " 再进⾏⼀次上
调 " 或许是合适的。
Evercore ISI 分析师指出，杰斐逊 “已经确认了纽约联储主席威廉姆斯传递的信息：美联储预计不
会在即将到来的 10 ⽉会议上连续加息” ，并认为在沃什主席⼏乎不提供利率前景指引的背景
下， " 杰斐逊与威廉姆斯的联合表态具有权威性 " 。
SGH Macro ⾸席美国经济学家 Tim Duy 此前在威廉姆斯发⾔后亦表⽰， “ 威廉姆斯需要表达得异
常清晰，因为市场对加息的定价已经偏离了美联储的实际意图。 ”
值得注意的是，负责⾦融监管的美联储副主席鲍曼（ Michelle Bowman ）当⽇也在华盛顿
Atlantic Council 的活动上释放了类似信号，表⽰ “ 我⽬前没有看到采取进⼀步⾏动的迫切需要 ” ，
并强调在上⽉加息之后， “ 我们需要⼀些时间来理解其如何在经济中传导 ” 。

杰斐逊、威廉姆斯与鲍曼均拥有 FOMC 永久投票权，三⼈的协调⽴场进⼀步巩固了市场对 10 ⽉
暂停加息的预期。
鹰派声⾳未绝，但难撼⼤局
尽管美联储⾼层释放鸽派信号，多位鹰派官员当⽇仍坚持强硬⽴场，但其影响⼒明显受限。
洛根在周四晚间的讲话中措辞最为强硬。她将 9 ⽉加息定性为 “ 第⼀步 ” ，并表⽰⾄少还需要再加
息 50 个基点，即两次 25 基点的加息，才能确保通胀回归 2% ⽬标。她指出，这些额外加息加上 9
⽉的⾏动，不过是抵消美联储去年为防范劳动⼒市场⼤幅降温⽽实施的三次降息 —— ⽽那种降
温并未出现。
Logan 还区分了债券收益率上⾏的不同驱动因素：若收益率上升反映市场对美联储更激进⾏动的
预期，则 " 不能替代我们的⼯作 " ；但若上升源于期限溢价，则 " 可以减缓经济，降低进⼀步收紧
货币政策的必要性 " 。
卡什卡利则在接受路透采访时表⽰，对于下⼀次加息是否应在 10 ⽉进⾏， “ 我没有强烈倾向 ” ，但
其当前预测仍包含今年再加息⼀次、明年再加息⼀次。他警告称：

Kashkari 还在彭博电视采访中特别指出，当前 2 年期美债收益率与美联储经济预测摘要（ SEP ）
隐含的 2 年期收益率之间存在历史性⼤幅背离，认为 “ 市场在想发出信号时，往往会发出信号 ” 。
波⼠顿联储主席柯林斯（ Susan Collins ）、堪萨斯城联储主席施密德（ Jeff Schmid ）以及⾥⼠
满联储主席巴尔⾦（ Thomas Barkin ）当⽇亦发表鹰派⾔论，均强调在劳动⼒市场相对稳健的背
景下，货币政策应聚焦于将通胀恢复⾄ 2% ⽬标。
“ 我们在制定政策时，没有奢侈的条件去孤⽴地看待这些冲击中的某⼀个，⽽必须考虑这⼀连串
冲击可能会如何影响整个经济。 ”
“ 如果经济被证明具有极强韧性，进⽽意味着通胀可能⽐我意识到的更具黏性，那么政策利率可
能需要升⾄⽐我⽬前预期更⾼的⽔平。 ”

### Page 3

但当被问及年内最后两次会议的政策⾛向时，所有官员均拒绝作答， Barkin 表⽰这将构成前瞻指
引，⽽ FOMC 已 " 明确表⽰ " 不打算提供此类指引。
市场反应：加息预期骤降，国债收益率回落
杰斐逊讲话的市场冲击⽴竿⻅影。据 CME Group 数据， 10 ⽉加息概率在杰斐逊发⾔后进⼀步跌
⾄约 25% ，⽽本周初这⼀数字还⾼达 70% 。全球主要券商⽬前普遍预期美联储今年仅再加息⼀
次，时间窗⼝为 12 ⽉⽽⾮ 10 ⽉。市场对 12 ⽉加息 25 基点的定价概率约为 61% ，加息 50 基点的概
率约为 18% 。
美国国债市场同步⾛强。 10 年期美债收益率从触及的 24 年⾼位约 5.25% 回落，短端收益率跌幅
更为显著，反映出市场对近期加息预期的快速重新定价。 10 年期美债收益率⾃美联储 9 ⽉ 16 ⽇加
息以来已从 5% 攀升⾄约 5.25% ，⻓端利率的上⾏本⾝具有收紧⾦融条件的效果，在⼀定程度上
替代了美联储直接加息的作⽤。
值得关注的是，当⽇美债的买盘并⾮完全由美联储表态驱动。法国和意⼤利国债市场当⽇出现

剧烈动荡，法国 OAT 利差⼤幅⾛阔，法国主权 CDS 急剧攀升，引发投资者⼤规模转向美债避
险。

### Page 4

RBC Capital Markets 美国利率策略师 Izaac Brook 表⽰， " 今天的⾛势在很⼤程度上与美国基本
⾯和美国数据⽆关，所有⼈都在看海外收益率，并认为需要转向安全资产、买⼊美债。 "
9 ⽉加息后的政策⼗字路⼝
美联储在 9 ⽉ 15 ⽇⾄ 16 ⽇的会议上⼀致投票决定加息 25 个基点，将联邦基⾦利率⽬标区间上调⾄
3.75% ⾄ 4.00% ，这是⾃ 2023 年以来的⾸次加息。决策者的预测中位数显⽰，他们⽀持今年再加
息⼀次、明年再加息⼀次。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

2 条评论
杰斐逊在讲话中将近期加息定性为确保⻓期通胀预期保持良好锚定的重要⼀步，同时指出经济
活动和劳动⼒市场依然保持稳固。他承认通胀居⾼不下的时间已经过⻓，并认为通胀持续处于
⾼位的⻛险依然存在，但强调在多重冲击叠加的背景下，政策制定需要更加审慎。
华尔街⻅闻⽂章写道，周三公布的 8 ⽉ PCE 数据显⽰，个⼈消费⽀出价格指数同⽐上涨 3.4% ，与
7 ⽉持平，仍远⾼于美联储 2% 的⽬标，但未进⼀步恶化。这⼀数据在威廉姆斯发⾔的基础上进
⼀步压低了 10 ⽉加息预期，为杰斐逊的鸽派表态提供了数据⽀撑。
下⼀个关键数据节点为周五（ 10 ⽉ 2 ⽇）公布的 9 ⽉⾮农就业报告。美联储官员此前表⽰，在劳
动⼒市场保持稳定的背景下，他们有空间将政策重⼼集中于压制通胀，这意味着就业数据⼤概

率不会再度颠覆利率政策预期。
此外，美联储主席沃什定于 10 ⽉ 16 ⽇在国际货币基⾦组织秋季年会上出席⼀场对话活动，这将
是美联储进⼊ 10 ⽉ 27 ⽇⾄ 28 ⽇会议前传统静默期前的最后⼀次公开表态机会，市场将密切关注
其是否会强化或修正本周传递的信号。

From the broad market to the 
 (
A
)
Iceberg  
What is the right price for carrying the 
 tail risk of the AI build
 -
out? 
W
e put th
 at 
question to structural credit models  
that 
price default risk from the equity market and the balance sheet
 , 
rather than from credit markets themselves
 . 
By definition,  
equity keeps the upside if the 
 AI 
build
 -
out works, credit 
mainly 
 absorbs the loss if it does not. 
 The mega
 -
cap technology complex already dominates the equity indices and is now ballooning in credit to finance that build
 -
out
. 
A
nd investment
 -
grade spreads offer only modest compensation  
of 
~
80

 ,

 fter the tech

2026.

The starting point is Merton (1974), and behind the mathematics sits a simple idea
 : 
a 
company owns assets and 
owes debt. If the assets are worth more than the debt when the bill comes due, shareholders keep the difference; if 
they are not, creditors take what is left. Owning the shares is therefore a bet on the assets clearing the debt 
 – 
and 
that means the equity market is already saying something about default risk. Two things drive the answer: how 
much cushion sits be
 tween what a company is worth and what it owes, and how fast that cushion can move, which 
is what equity volatility measures. A thick cushion that barely moves is safe. A thin one that swings around is not.  
The model implicitly assumes 
 the consensus  
AI narrative  
embedded in equity
 , 
with 
equity volatility taken as a proxy 
for the market's uncertainty.  
This approach 
 may not fully appreciate the 
 income from 
 current AI
 -
investment
 . 
B
ut 
credit investors will barely benefit  
from it, 
and 
 instead 
 need to focus on do
 wnside against 
 slim spread.

Merton’s limitation is that it is too well
 -
behaved at the top end. Give it a company with 2% leverage and it reports a 
default risk of essentially zero 
 – 
hard to argue 
 and 
 work with. 
A 
KMV
 -
inspired 
 approach
 1 
– 
our own implementation 
of the published structural framework, not the proprietary Moody’s Analytics KMV  
model 
 – 
keeps that logic and 
fixes the last step: instead of reading default risk off a bell curve, it reads it off what has actually happened 
 in the 
past 
 to companies 
 with similar cushions
 . 
I
t 
also 
 assumes a 
 that 
 firm has to cover its short
 -
term debt plus 
 part of  
its 
long
 -
term debt
 , 
rather than everything  
all  
at once. The output arrives in a language credit investors already use 
 – 
an implied rating. That matters here, because when Merton has flattened to zero, the rating still moves. 
CreditGrades, a variant that allows for uncertainty about exactly where the d
 efault point sits, is run alongside as a 
sanity check.

We run 
both 
models  
on 
key technology 
 companies  
– 
Microsof
 t, Apple, Alphabet, Amazon, Meta
 , 
Nvidia, 
 and  
Oracle
 . 
SpaceX  
was also 
 added 
 in 
recent analysis
 , completing the eight
 -
name panel we refer to throughout
 . 
The analysis 
covers 
 five timestamps from mid
 -
2022 
 – 
the onset of the AI theme 
 – 
to mid
 -
2026, using the market 
 capitalization
 , 
equity volatility and risk
 -
free rate prevailing at each date. Each name is priced under two 
 debt  
barriers: 
 reported 
balance
 -
sheet 
financial debt only, 
 versus  
balance
 -

 off
-

commitments

together
 ,

(

On the 
 recognized

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN DWELLING

Unit approvals drive the headline lower
30 September 2026
Key points
• Total dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in
August, with the annual pace easing to
10.3%yr. The headline decline was driven
by a sharp fall in private unit approvals.

• Private detached dwelling approvals
rose 3.7%mth, reaching their highest
level since August 2021. In contrast,
private unit approvals fell 21.2%mth.
• Queensland recorded the weakest
result, while NSW also posted
a sizeable decline. By contrast,
WA, Victoria and South Australia
recorded gains over the month.
• The value of residential building
approvals fell 0.6%mth. Volatility
in the data centre dominated ‘other
commercial buildings’ segment
contributed to the total value of

building approvals falling 21.3%mth.
• While detached dwelling approvals
continue to show resilience, recent
RBA cash rate increases continue
to present risks to the outlook.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the
annual pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. The result was broadly
in line with Westpac’s expectation for a 5.0%mth decline,
although notably weaker than the market expectation for a
1.0%mth fall. July’s decline in private detached dwellings

proved short lived, with approvals rebounding in August.
Instead, weakness in the headline result was driven by a
sizeable decline in private unit approvals.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals rose 3.7%mth in August,
following a large 2.5ppt upward revision to July’s 1.9%mth
decline. The increase was the strongest in more than a year,

with private detached dwelling approvals reaching their highest
level since August 2021. With the annual pace strengthening
to 18.4%yr, detached dwelling approvals continue to appear
resilient despite recent interest rate increases. Some of this
resilience may partly reflect the implementation of changes to
SMSF property borrowing arrangements on 10 August.
Private unit approvals drove the decline in the headline result,
falling 21.2%mth in August. Following a 1.2ppt upward

revision to July’s 0.8%mth increase, the annual pace returned to
contractionary territory at –2.1%yr. Weakness was broad-based
across segments, with both high-rise and low-mid-rise
approvals declining in the month.
Queensland recorded the weakest result, with private approvals
falling 22.6%mth, driven primarily by weakness in unit approvals,
while private approvals in NSW also declined, falling 17.1%mth.
By contrast, WA (+0.2%mth), Victoria (+8.4%mth) and South

Australia (+32.0%mth) all recorded gains over the month.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 0.6%mth,
driven by a 0.5%mth decline in new residential approvals
and a 2.1%mth fall in renovation activity. Non-residential
approvals declined sharply, down 44.8%mth. Volatility
continues to be driven by the data centre-dominated ‘other
commercial’ segment, which fell 91.1%mth on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. Excluding this segment, in non-seasonally

adjusted terms, non-residential approvals fell a more subdued
13.9%mth (from -37.9%mth). The total value of building
approvals declined 21.3%mth in August.
August’s result suggests dwelling approvals continue to
moderate following the strength recorded earlier in the year.
However, the underlying picture remains more resilient, with
the headline decline driven by volatility in unit approvals
while detached dwellings strengthened. NSW and Victoria,

traditionally among the more interest rate sensitive housing
markets, recorded the weakest detached dwelling outcomes,
potentially signalling broader rate effects ahead. For a
comprehensive assessment of housing market conditions, see
our latest Housing Pulse (see here).
Detached approvals point to some resilience

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, 20.45 điểm tương ứng 1.14%, với thanh khoản cải

tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị 2,680 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, HDB và BSR; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở 
mức 1,172 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại mua ròng 3,009 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 5,001 hợp đồng. Tự doanh bán ròng -9,259 hợp đồng và đang duy 
trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng 9,458 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần tăng điểm tích cực, chỉ số VNINDEX chinh phục thành công 
ngưỡng tâm lý 1.800 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện, độ rộng thị trường nghiêng về số mã 
tăng điểm và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài quay trở lại mua ròng. Động lực hỗ trợ thị trường 
trong tuần qua đến từ hoạt động cơ cấu của các quỹ bám theo rổ chỉ số mới nổi của FTSE, 
khi mà ngày 21/9 là ngày Việt Nam được chính thức nâng hạng. Thêm vào đó, diễn biến hạ 
nhiệt của giá dầu khi lo ngại thiếu hụt cung do xung đột ở Trung Đông giảm bớt cũng là 
yếu tố hỗ trợ tâm lý thị trường chung. Ở chiều ngược lại, thông tin FED tăng lãi suất có tác 
động tiêu cực nhưng ảnh hưởng không đáng kể lên thị trường do động thái này đã được 
dự báo từ trước.

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

Kết quả kinh doanh 2Q2026 tích cực 
nhờ hưởng lợi từ căng thẳng địa 
chính trị ở Trung Đông

Giá dầu duy trì ở mức cao, tiếp tục hỗ 
trợ biên lợi nhuận khí khô của GAS

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi mở rộng dư địa 
phát triển mỏ cận biên, tạo động lực 
tăng nguồn khí cho GAS trong dài 
hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
96,300 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng trưởng tích cực 44% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ (1) giá bán khí tăng 
và (2) tăng trưởng sản lượng tích cực từ mảng LNG. Trong khi đó, LNST-CĐTS tăng 
chậm hơn, ở mức 23% YoY, do ghi nhận khoản hoàn nhập dự phòng nợ phải thu khó 
đòi thấp hơn so với cùng kỳ năm trước.

Giá dầu Brent tăng trở lại lên vùng 95 USD/thùng trong bối cảnh xung đột Mỹ - Iran 
tiếp tục leo thang, làm gián đoạn nguồn cung dầu tại Trung Đông, trong khi lưu 
lượng tàu qua Eo biển Hormuz vẫn ở mức thấp và tồn kho dầu toàn cầu cùng Kho 
Dự trữ Chiến lược Mỹ liên tục suy giảm. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá dầu duy trì vùng 85 - 
95 USD/thùng trong ngắn hạn, qua đó tiếp tục hỗ trợ biên lợi nhuận mảng khí 
thượng nguồn của GAS nhờ chênh lệch giữa giá bán khí theo giá dầu và giá miệng 
giếng cố định tại các mỏ lâu đời.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi tăng ưu đãi cho các mỏ cận biên, giúp cải thiện hiệu quả đầu 
tư và thúc đẩy phát triển nguồn khí mới. GAS có thể hưởng lợi từ sản lượng khí bổ 
sung qua hệ thống hạ tầng hiện hữu, nhưng tác động chủ yếu trong trung và dài 
hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA để định giá cổ phiếu GAS, chúng tôi đưa 
ra khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 96,300 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức 
upside 16.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Kết quả kinh doanh tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Giá urea hạ nhiệt, xuất khẩu tiếp tục 
là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính 
của DCM trong 2H2026

Giá khí đầu vào DCM 2028–2030F 
tăng do bổ sung nguồn khí giá cao từ 
Nam Du – U Minh

Luật Dầu khí mới cải thiện triển vọng 
nguồn khí đầu vào cho DCM trong 
dài hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
42,100 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng 10% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ giá bán urea tăng trong bối cảnh thị 
trường chịu tác động từ các yếu tố địa chính trị. LNST-CĐTS tăng 36% YoY, được hỗ 
trợ bởi giá bán đầu ra tăng nhanh hơn chi phí khí đầu vào.

Giá urea thế giới sau khi tăng cao trong Q2/2026 đã điều chỉnh về vùng trước chiến 
tranh do nhu cầu suy yếu và nguồn cung Trung Quốc quay trở lại. Đối với DCM, xuất 
khẩu urea tiếp tục là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính, trong khi giá và sản lượng 
urea nội địa được kỳ vọng đi ngang trong 2H2026 do đây không phải mùa cao điểm 
tiêu thụ trong nước.

Chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng 10% dự báo giá khí đầu vào DCM giai đoạn 2028–2030F, 
phản ánh việc bổ sung nguồn khí Nam Du – U Minh từ Q4/2028. Giá khí Nam Du – U 
Minh dự kiến 11.56 USD/mmBTU, cao hơn ~35% so với các nguồn hiện hữu, tạo áp 
lực tăng chi phí đầu vào.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi sẽ rút ngắn quy trình phê duyệt FDP và bổ sung ưu đãi cho các 
mỏ khó khai thác, qua đó hỗ trợ đầu tư E&P và duy trì sản lượng dầu khí trong 
nước. Đối với DCM, tác động dự kiến mang tính gián tiếp và có độ trễ, chủ yếu thông 
qua cải thiện nguồn cung khí nội địa, chưa phải động lực thúc đẩy lợi nhuận đáng 
kể trong ngắn hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu DCM chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 42,100 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
29.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Doanh thu và LNST-CĐTS 2Q2026 
tiếp tục tăng trưởng tốt, được hỗ trợ 
bởi đà tăng của crack spread

Biên lợi nhuận duy trì ở mức cao 
trong ngắn hạn nhờ crack spread 
bùng nổ, nhưng có thể biến động 
mạnh

Biên lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn chịu 
áp lực từ rủi ro dư cung dầu

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
32,200 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Q2/2026, doanh thu BSR tăng 60% YoY nhờ giá nhiên liệu tăng mạnh trong 
bối cảnh nguồn cung gián đoạn do xung đột Trung Đông. LNST-CĐTS tăng 807% 
YoY, được hỗ trợ bởi crack spread của bốn sản phẩm lọc dầu chủ lực tăng mạnh do 
nguồn cung thắt chặt.

Xung đột quân sự Mỹ – Iran gây gián đoạn nghiêm trọng dòng chảy dầu qua Eo biển 
Hormuz, thắt chặt nguồn cung dầu thô và thành phẩm, đẩy giá Brent lên quanh 100 
USD/thùng và đưa crack spread tại châu Á đạt đỉnh và duy trì ở vùng cao từ tháng 3 
đến nay. Diễn biến crack spread thời gian tới sẽ phụ thuộc đáng kể vào diễn biến 
chiến sự tại Eo biển Hormuz và tốc độ khôi phục lưu thông qua Eo biển này.

Triển vọng lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn của BSR chịu áp lực khi cán cân cung – cầu 
dầu toàn cầu được dự báo chuyển sang dư cung trong trung và dài hạn. Tuy nhiên, 
nhu cầu tái tích lũy tồn kho dầu toàn cầu có thể phần nào hấp thụ lượng cung dư 
thừa và hạn chế mức độ thu hẹp crack spread.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu BSR chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị Mua với mức giá mục tiêu 32,200 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
16.9% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026. Chúng tôi áp dụng mức EV/EBITDA mục 
tiêu là 15.8 lần, bằng với mức chuẩn trung bình 5 năm của cổ phiếu.

BÁO CÁO NGẮN CƠ HỘI ĐẦU TƯ

CTCP Nhựa Tiền Phong (NTP) 
Tăng tốc trong chu kỳ thuận lợi

Mua 
Giá mục tiêu VND 73.200 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 1 50.000 – 51.400 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 1 44% 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 2 42.000 – 44.000 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 2 70%

Lưu ý: 
Nhà đầu tư cần kết hợp nhận định xu hướng thị 
trường chung, mức độ hấp dẫn của các điểm 
nhấn đầu tư và khẩu vị rủi ro của mình để quản 
lý tỷ trọng tại từng điểm mua cho phù hợp. 
Do việc xác định mỗi điểm mua được dựa trên 
các khung thời gian khác nhau nên chúng tôi 
không đưa ra mức dừng lỗ cố định. Nhìn chung, 
mức dừng lỗ nên được đặt chặt hơn cho điểm 
mua gần (5-7%) và rộng hơn cho điểm mua xa 
(10-15%).

ĐIỂM NHẤN ĐẦU TƯ 
Kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ tăng trưởng mạnh hơn so với giai đoạn trước. KBSV 
dự báo sản lượng tiêu thụ của NTP tăng 2,3% CAGR giai đoạn 2026 - 2028 nhờ (1) 
Đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư hạ tầng tại miền Bắc (2) gia tăng đầu tư hạ tầng tại các 
địa phương thu hút FDI (3) tăng tốc đơn hàng mảng dự án.

Giá hạt nhựa sẽ gây áp lực biên lợi nhuận nửa cuối 2026 nhưng không làm thay 
đổi triển vọng dài hạn. Mặc dù duy trì chính sách dự phòng nguyên liệu cao và đa 
dạng hóa nhà cung cấp, nhưng NTP vẫn đối mặt với rủi ro thu hẹp lợi nhuận, 
nhưng sẽ không làm giảm đáng kể triển vọng tăng trưởng trung hạn. KBSV cho 
rằng tình trạng dư cung hạt nhựa tại Trung Quốc vẫn sẽ duy trì trong trung hạn và 
xung đột được giải quyết, giảm áp lực biên lợi nhuận toàn ngành.

Catalyst thoái vốn kỳ vọng tái định giá doanh nghiệp. Theo kế hoạch thoái vốn 
Nhà nước, SCIC dự kiến thoái toàn bộ 37,1% cổ phần tại NTP . Chúng tôi đánh giá 
việc hiện thực hóa thương vụ có thể giúp NTP (1) thu hút nhà đầu tư chiến lược 
mới; (2) cộng hưởng mô hình kinh doanh; (3) tái định giá doanh nghiệp.

Hoạt động kinh doanh  Diễn biến doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất

NTP là một trong những doanh nghiệp đầu 
ngành ống nhựa tại Việt Nam, với thị phần 
khoảng 31% toàn quốc và 60% tại miền Bắc. 
Hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi tập trung vào 
sản xuất và phân phối ống nhựa cùng các sản 
phẩm phụ tùng. Nhờ lợi thế về thương hiệu, 
quy mô và mạng lưới phân phối, NTP duy trì 
vị thế dẫn đầu và nền tảng tăng trưởng ổn 
định qua các năm.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KB Securities Vietnam

Tương quan mặt bằng giá  Diễn biến P/E của doanh nghiệp

NTP đang được giao dịch với mức P/E khoảng 
8,3 lần thấp hơn trung bình 5 năm của cổ 
phiếu. Với kỳ vọng doanh nghiệp sẽ bước vào 
pha tăng trưởng mạnh trong 3 năm tới, chúng 
tôi cho rằng P/E fw 2026F ở mức 9,5x lần phù 
hợp với mức dự phóng tăng trưởng 11,6% 
CAGR giai đoạn 2026 – 2028.

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín d ụng 2026 đ ạt 27%, với động lực chính 
đến từ phân khúc KHDN, đ ặc biệt là t ừ nhóm FDI trong b ối cảnh dòng v ốn FDI vào 
Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực. Xét về lĩnh vực, cho vay kinh 
doanh BĐS kỳ vọng sẽ tăng trưởng vượt trội khi ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp vốn 
cho các dự án hạ tầng và d ự án BĐS ưu tiên , trong khi các m ảng kinh doanh khác 
tăng trưởng ở mức khả quan.

Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM giảm so với năm 2025 do áp l ực huy động khiến chi 
phí vốn duy trì ở mặt bằng cao, trong khi lãi su ất cho vay dự kiến ở mức cạnh tranh 
để ưu tiên mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng.

Chúng tôi dự phóng tỷ lệ nợ xấu của ngân hàng sẽ tăng 11bps YoY trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất đang neo ở mức cao và kỳ vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì chính sách trích 
lập dự phòng thận trọng nhằm củng cố bộ đệm, qua đó hoàn thành mục tiêu duy 
trì tỷ lệ bao phủ nợ xấu ở mức trên 100%.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu MBB với giá mục tiêu 25,950 
VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 26.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 17/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX cho thấy xu hướng hồi phục trong tuần qua nhưng với diễn biến khá giằng co, 
được thể hiện qua sự hình thành của một mẫu nến "spinning" trên đồ thị tuần. Trên khung 
ngày, bức tranh có phần tích cực hơn khi chỉ số đã cho phản ứng với vùng hỗ trợ quanh 
1790 và hồi phục trở lại, phản ánh sự nhập cuộc khá chủ động của lực cầu. Vùng kháng cự 
gần quanh 1850 bắt đầu khiến áp lực bán có phần gia tăng trong phiên cuối tuần và diễn 
biến rung lắc có thể sẽ còn tiếp diễn. Mặc dù vậy, khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được 
bảo lưu, nhịp giảm điểm vừa qua nhiều khả năng chỉ là bước điều chỉnh tích lũy trước khi 
VNINDEX thử thách lại và có cơ hội vượt vùng đỉnh cũ. 
Kháng cự xa: 1900 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1850 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1770 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1715 (+-15)

- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua.

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

4 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we
1 Source: Bloomberg as of September 8, 2026.
2 Source: Bloomberg as of September 9, 2026.

Investment Implications
The recent rise in Treasury yields
reflects a collision of fiscal concerns,
resilient growth, persistent inflation,
and record debt issuance from both
the Treasury and corporate borrowers.
While this has created periodic bouts
of market pressures, we view recent
move as a normalization process, not a
crisis of confidence. Attractive real and
nominal yields reinforce our view that
investors should look beyond cash and
short-duration assets and move closer

to strategic duration target.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

6 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to cconcerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to

other Government bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason for yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still above average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

Document 206: Cliff Notes_ searching for signal (2026-10-03)

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
13:33 October 02 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the
cash rate by 25bps to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given Governor Bullock had
already communicated that upside risks to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established by

the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.

### Page 2

Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks. The Middle East conflict was emphasised as
the risk that had “materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude oil and refined
product prices over recent months. The RBA’s liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of
costs and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic capacity pressures and the data
centre/AI investment boom were also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.

In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in
November is low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided there is no sudden resolution
of the Middle East conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the outlook for
energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying (trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr),
which was slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s forecast. As usual, the

monthly detail was mixed, but the key drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-related
components, particularly rents and new dwelling purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with
the unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel prices. We have maintained our forecast
for a 0.9% lift in trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building for Q4, a follow-up rate
hike in November appears most likely.

Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending surprised with a flat reading in August.
Services spend fell back following recent strength that was driven by major sporting events, but this
was offset by an increase in goods spend, particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending pulse is shading a touch softer. However,
another solid outcome for business investment could once again be on the cards for Q3. August’s

goods trade data showcased another significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our modelling suggests that the local
economic impact of such investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s latest update revealed that dwelling prices
fell another 1.1% nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have led the correction to

date, the fact that the other capitals are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest rates collide with the tax policy changes
announced in May’s budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced by the continued pull-
back in investor credit growth. Westpac anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For more, see our latest Housing Pulse.

Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength in household demand and higher energy
prices. Headline PCE inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased 0.2%mth and
3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%,
suggesting some easing in broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household spending

remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal
income growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling households opted to draw down savings.

### Page 3

The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the headline suggested. While the index edged down
0.1pt to 54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive months. There were some signs
of capacity constraints emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs up 4.6pts to 56.4,
suggesting that firms struggled to keep pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs increased. Firms responded by lifting

employment, 1.5pts to 52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed acceleration in cost
pressures, with the prices paid index jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every
major industry reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in

this week’s busy Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes, citing strong inflation,
diminished supply capacity and policy that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing to
progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time to assess upcoming data. The one thing
both agree on is that inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and extent of further rate
hikes. Our view remains that one further hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.

In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted support measures aimed at stabilising
growth and supporting the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its Pledged
Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and expanded quotas for targeted lending programs.
Authorities also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The subsidy applies to homes with
a floor area of 120m² or less and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of mortgage

costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of five years. The measures complement reforms
announced in August to improve developer financing conditions and support housing completion.
However, weak credit demand and confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy
transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement in activity, although the details were less
convincing. Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts to 50.1, supported by

stronger production and new orders. Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in prices,
with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market
conditions remained weak, with the employment index falling a further 0.6pts and remaining below 50
for almost four years. Conditions in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the services PMI
lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger construction activity. Input and output prices moved

higher, selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved to 48.5 and employment
increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some traction, but weak labour demand,
subdued credit growth and soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another constructive signal on the economy and

reinforced the case for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved by 2pts for large
manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while profitability indicators strengthened and capital
expenditure intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect fixed investment to increase by
11.6% in FY2026, while large non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%, pointing to

### Page 4

Related articles
continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with
measures of excess production capacity becoming less negative and indicating that demand remains
sufficiently strong to keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also intensified, with
employment conditions becoming more negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a

progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations remained anchored around 2%, providing
further evidence that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation environment. Overall, the
survey was consistent with an economy operating close to capacity and supports the view that a
December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

Document 231: Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas

### Page 1

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.

to a 3-year high

Six of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in revenues relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest revenue growth rate for the quarter, while the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest revenue growth rate (43.8%) of any sector. It is expected to generate sales of 
€310.9B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €216.2B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (-15.4%) of any sector. It is expected to generate sales of 
€1.8B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €2.1B in Q3 2025.

world
NEXT GENERATION EU: THE PROGRAMME IS COMING TO AN END, BUT ITS EFFECTS ON ECONOMIC ACTIVITY WILL
ENDURE
Guillaume Derrien
A pillar of the NextGenerationEU programme, the Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) will expire on December 31, 2026. As of the
end of August 2026, slightly more than three-quarters of the total budget had been disbursed (EUR 440 billion out of EUR 573 billion).
Disbursing the remaining quarter (EUR 133 billion) by the end of the year will require a significant increase compared with the histo-

rical pace (EUR 90 billion per year). The goal of channeling these funds toward productive investment appears to have been achieved.
Tangible results for investment in Europe
According to Eurostat, public spending co-financed by the RRF totaled EUR 310 billion between 2020 and 2025. Nearly three-quarters (74%)
consisted of capital expenditures, including EUR 106 billion in direct public investment and EUR 122 billion in capital transfers to the private

sector (such as grants). This spending supported investment by businesses and households.
The list of major programmes funded by the RFF, published by the European Commission 1, shows that, within this scope, nearly two-thirds of
the funding went to transportation (primarily rail), energy infrastructure, and digital infrastructure (see Chart 1).
Another notable point: capital expenditures co-financed by the RRF increased significantly, reaching 0.4% of GDP for the European Union as a

whole in 2025 (see Chart 2). There are significant disparities among Member States, primarily due to differences in the amounts allocated to
them. Croatia (1.9%), Greece (1.2%), Italy (1%), and Spain (0.7%) received significant support, in contrast to France and Germany (0.1%). These
figures are consistent with the IMF’s estimate that the RRF added approximately 0.3 percentage points (pp) to EU growth in 2025 (more than
1 pp in Greece and Croatia, around 0.8 pp in Italy, and 0.5 pp in Spain) 2. Furthermore, as the first major joint European borrowing initiative, the

RRF made it possible to finance investment without increasing national budget deficits, giving several countries (Spain, Italy, Greece, Portugal)
valuable breathing room to consolidate their public finances.
1 Recovery and Resilience Scoreboard
2 The EU’s Recovery and Resilience Facility: Experience and Lessons for the Next Multiannual Financial Framework, WP/26/114, June 2026
SOURCE: EUROPEAN COMMISSION, Recovery and Resilience Scorecard
Infrastructure spending accounts for the majority of the funding

Chart of the Week / 23 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
What impact on EU’s economic activity can we expect once this programme ends?
At first glance, effects will be limited. The end of the programme is expected to weigh on both public and private investment. However, the
strengthening of economic activity that we anticipate (see details in our latest Ecoperspectives – Q3 2026) rests primarily on German public

investment programmes and structural drivers (investment in AI, defence spending).
The ECB makes the same point in its September macroeconomic projections 3. It emphasises that the end of the RRF is expected to weigh
on public investment in 2027, while it anticipates an acceleration in business investment. Furthermore, RRF funds are being absorbed on
the ground only gradually. Since most disbursements are concentrated toward the end of the programme, the effects of the RRF will extend

beyond 2026.
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

Document 294: Prospect of Accelerating Wage Growth In A Strong Economy

Source PDF: all report pdf/Prospect of Accelerating Wage Growth In A Strong Economy.pdf
Report source: Substack Reports
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Prospect of Accelerating Wage Growth In A Strong Economy
Date:    5 hours ago
Prospect of Accelerating Wage Growth In A Strong Economy

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Prospect of Accelerating Wage
Growth In A Strong Economy
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 29 Sept 2026
View in browser
Photo by Anton Filatov / Unsplash

I. Is Wage Growth Accelerating?
Wage growth signals labor market conditions. Just as shortages of goods
tend to push prices higher, worker shortages tend to push wages higher.
When labor demand outstrips labor supply, employers must compete more
aggressively for workers, resulting in faster wage growth.
Former Fed Chair Jerome Powell's framework for assessing labor-market
tightness compares labor demand, using the sum of household

### Page 2

employment plus job openings, with the labor force (chart). By that
measure, demand has slightly exceeded supply for four straight months,
the longest such streak since March 2023. Powell's measure of excessive
labor demand correlates positively with the Atlanta Fed's Wage Growth
Tracker, which may be starting to increase (chart).
The Wage Growth Tracker rose to 4.1% y/y in August, the fastest pace
since September 2025 (chart). Wage growth for job switchers accelerated

to 5.0%, the strongest since June 2024.
Average hourly earnings growth slowed to 3.1% y/y in August, the
weakest pace since 2021 (chart). We place greater weight on the Wage
Growth Tracker because it is less affected by workforce composition
shifts. For example, the retirement of higher-paid Baby Boomers can

### Page 3

depress average hourly earnings even when underlying wage growth
remains firm.
So what's the verdict? The labor market isn't tight enough yet to trigger a
significant acceleration in wage growth. However, labor demand has been
firming while labor supply is being constrained by tighter immigration
policies and the ongoing retirement of Baby Boomers (chart). As a result,
wage growth is likely to move higher as labor-market conditions continue
to tighten.

A sustained pickup in wage growth would add to the list of inflationary
pressures that already includes energy, tariffs, and the AI buildout. For
now, the good news is that unit labor cost (ULC) inflation rose just 1.4%
y/y in Q2 (chart). ULC is the ratio of hourly compensation to productivity.

### Page 4

II. US Economy
Recent economic indicators continue to confirm that the US economy is
booming. That's great for corporate earnings. On the other hand, it is one
reason bond yields are rising. In our view, bond yields are not currently
high enough to slow the economy, which is less interest-rate sensitive
than it was in the not-so-distant past.
Consider the following:
(1) JOLTS. Job openings fell to 7.1 million in August, the lowest since
March (chart). We wouldn't read too much into the monthly decline,

however, given the series' volatility. The six-month moving average rose to
its highest level since March 2023 and has increased for five straight
months. Similarly, 35% of small businesses reported unfilled positions in
August, well above the historical average of 24%.

### Page 5

Contrary to the widely accepted "no-hires-no-fires" description of the labor
market, hires totaled 5.2 million in August, slightly exceeding separations
(layoffs plus quits) (chart).
Indeed job postings turned positive on a y/y basis for the first time in four
years, suggesting that labor-market activity continued to strengthen in
September (chart).

### Page 6

(2) Consumer confidence. How do consumers perceive the current state
of the labor market? September's Conference Board data show 54.5% of
respondents say jobs are available, above the long-run average of 48.3%
(chart). Those saying jobs are hard to get rose from 20.3% to 21.9%.
(3) Consumer spending. As expected given the ongoing strength in the
labor market, consumer spending remains robust. Redbook same-store
retail sales rose 8.2% y/y during the week ended September 25,

remaining well above the 2025 average of 5.8% (chart).

### Page 7

(4) Manufacturing. Manufacturing activity remains strong too. The
average of the five regional Fed manufacturing surveys remained elevated
at 13.4 in September, suggesting that the national ISM M-PMI likely
remained solidly in expansion territory during the month (chart).
Meanwhile, the average prices paid and received measures rose to their
highest levels since 2022, indicating that upward pressure on consumer
prices is likely to persist (chart).

### Page 8

III. Fed Speak
Not surprisingly given the latest news, two voting FOMC members
recently made hawkish comments.
New York Fed President John Williams said that "if the economy evolves
in a manner broadly consistent with my forecast, one further upward
adjustment of the federal funds target range may be appropriate late this
year." The comment suggests that Williams still expects another rate hike
but is willing to be patient, potentially waiting until December before

acting.
Similarly, Fed Governor Michael Barr said that "in my base case, further
policy adjustments are likely to be needed to ensure inflation comes down
to target in a timely fashion."
Our view remains that the Fed should at least reverse last year's three
25bps "insurance" cuts. When making those cuts, Fed policymakers
underestimated the economy's strength and were overly confident that
inflation was on a sustainable path back to the 2.0% y/y official target. Two

additional rate hikes would better align monetary policy with the current
balance of risks to the Fed's dual mandate.
💡
Join the discussion with Ed below! To leave comments or questions,
log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end

### Page 9

clause.

Document 297: Q3 Job Vacancies_ another step lower (2026-10-04)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Job Vacancies_ another step lower (2026-10-04).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-04
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #labour #employment
Q3 Job Vacancies: another step lower
By Neha Sharma
Economist, Westpac
13:07 October 01 2026 Share
The number of job vacancies fell by 3.0k (–0.9%qtr) between May and August. The decline
was wholly driven by the private sector, where vacancies fell 2.0%qtr. In contrast, public
sector vacancies rose 7.8%qtr.

### Page 2

Job vacancies fell by 3.0k (–0.9%qtr) between May and August, extending the previous quarter’s
decline. The May and February estimates were also revised slightly lower. As a result, vacancies are
now 1.4% below their level a year earlier. The result was at odds with partial indicators, which all
pointed to a rise in the quarter.

The decline was wholly driven by the private sector, where vacancies fell 2.0%qtr to be 1.5%yr lower.
In contrast, public sector vacancies rose 7.8%qtr, marking the strongest quarterly increase in four years
and partially reversing the sharp 7.5%qtr decline recorded in the May quarter. Public sector vacancies
were flat over the year.

Job vacancies have largely moved sideways since early 2025, though a resumption of the broader
downtrend now appears increasingly likely. Labour demand has held up reasonably well, though not
sufficiently to match growth in labour supply, resulting in a gradual easing in labour market conditions
(see here). Today’s result is also consistent with our view that this process will continue into early 2027
as the cumulative effects of high inflation and higher interest rates continue to weigh on activity and
hiring demand.

### Page 3

The vacancy-to-unemployment ratio, a key measure of labour market tightness closely monitored by
the RBA, declined from 0.48 to 0.45. The fall was the sharpest since early 2025, reflecting both a
decline in vacancies and a 6.8%qtr increase in the number of unemployed people over the quarter,
largely owing to the continued strength in labour supply. The ratio remains well above its long-run
average of around 0.2.

Given the strong growth in labour supply, the Beveridge curve provides a useful framework for
assessing recent labour market developments. By comparing vacancies and unemployment as shares
of the labour force, it controls for growth in the overall size of the labour market. Between 2022 and
2024, the labour market moved down the steep portion of the Beveridge curve, with substantial
declines in vacancies occurring alongside only modest increases in unemployment. While labour
market tightness has continued to unwind, Australia remains in this region of the curve, suggesting that
further rebalancing between labour demand and supply can occur through lower vacancies without a
material rise in unemployment.

### Page 4

All statistics mentioned below are not seasonally adjusted and hence may not add up to the seasonally
adjusted statistics referenced above.

By industry, job vacancies declined in eight of the 18 industries over the three months to August. The
largest falls were recorded in wholesale trade (–18.2%qtr), professional, scientific & technical services
(–17.4%qtr), and information media & telecommunications (–16.7%qtr). Consumer-facing sectors were
mixed, with retail trade (24.6%) and arts & recreation services (10.3%) recording strong increases,
while accommodation & food services edged 0.9% lower. Only three industries have seen vacancies
fall below their pre-pandemic levels: administrative & support services (–24.3%qtr), information media
& telecommunications (–9.1%qtr), and financial & insurance services (–7.3%qtr).

Overall market sector vacancies were flat in the quarter, meanwhile the non-market sector saw a 2.9%
drop, owing to declines in education & training (–8.2%qtr) and health care & social assistance (–
4.2%qtr). For detailed insights on industry condition, see Q2 Labour Account and the Westpac
Business Signal.

Document 329: Understanding the balance film industry a for larger AI chip (Part I)

### Page 1

From:    陸⾏之  Andrew Lu on global semis and techs <andrewhclu@substack.com>
Subject: Understanding the balance film industry a for larger AI chip (Part I)
Date:    6 hours ago
Understanding the balance film industry a for larger AI chip (Part I)

Understanding the balance
ﬁlm industry a for larger AI
chip (Part I)
應該是時候了解 AI ⼤晶⽚抗翹曲應⼒平衡膜產業鏈
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS
SEP 28 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP
Executive summary
雖然  Balance Film 產業還在初始冒芽階段，但當 AI GPU ， TPU ， ASIC 越來

越⼤顆， ABF 載板及 RDL 處理層數越來越厚，越來越多不同熱膨脹係數晶⽚及
材料被封裝在⼀起，防翹曲技術及抗翹曲應⼒平衡膜就越來越重要。當未來 10
年， AI 算⼒數據中⼼的投資將受限於可⽤資⾦，可⽤電⼒，可⽤⼟地，可⽤晶
⽚產能，除了發展困難度超⾼的衛星軌道數據中⼼，提供更⾼算⼒ / 更低耗能
（單位⾯積算⼒耗能⽐較，增加每單位數據中⼼⾯積的 GW 算⼒）的⼤型 AI 訓
練及推理晶⽚的騰籠換⿃原廠升級（舊機櫃換成新機櫃 + 光通訊  scale
out/scale across ），可能反⽽是⼀個可⾏的⽅向。
What is the balance ﬁlm?
Balance Film （抗翹曲應⼒平衡膜）在先進封裝領域中，是⼀種專⾨⽤來「抵
消」異質整合製程中因熱膨脹係數（ CTE ， Coeﬃcient of Thermal
Expansion ）不匹配所產⽣之應⼒，進⽽抑制載板與晶圓翹曲（ Warpage ）
的關鍵材料。
在進⾏  CoWoS, FOPLP （⾯板級扇出型封裝）或玻璃基板（ Glass

Substrate ）等⼤尺⼨封裝製程時，矽晶粒、矽 / 玻璃中介層， ABF 載板、玻璃
基板（ Glass core substrate) 、銅線路（ RDL ）以及環氧樹脂（ EMC ）的膨
脹與收縮率各不相同。經過⾼溫製程冷卻後，這種應⼒失衡會導致嚴重的翹曲
變形，造成後續微影對位偏移或基板破裂。 Balance Film 透過貼合或塗佈於

### Page 2

基板特定區域，在前段製程中主動形成反向拉扯的「對沖」應⼒，將基板維持
在平整狀態。
When the market started to use balance ﬁlm?
我們需要了解，⼤型  AI GPU/TPU 晶⽚  的  CoW 矽 / 玻璃中介層「有翹曲問
題」不代表現在都⼀定會⽤獨⽴的  Balance Film 。  Balance Film 是⼀種額
外的「反向應⼒補償材料」，主要在⼤尺⼨、⾼層數、薄型化、 RDL/PLP/ 先進
封裝製程翹曲變嚴重時導⼊，⽽不是標準必備層。
Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
Why a larger AI chip needs it?
Chip size matters
AI GPU/TPU 的矽中介層  Silicon Interposer 將⾛到 5.5x 光罩尺⼨，晶⽚⾯
積增加後，當台積電 AI GPU 客戶 Nvidia/AMD 及 AI ASIC/TPU 客戶亞⾺遜 / ⾕
歌 /Meta/ 微軟為了追求更⾼算⼒從 2028 年開始推疊邏輯電晶體及 12 顆

HBM4E 存儲記憶體，甚⾄ 20-24 顆 HBM5E 存儲記憶體，⼀⽚ 12“ 晶圓能切出
8 ⽚的 5.5x reticle size (70mmx70mm=4,900mm2) 晶⽚⼤⼩所需要的矽
中介層（ Silicon Interposer) ，將只能切出 5 ⽚  9x reticle size (90mm x

### Page 3

90mm=8,100mm2) 晶⽚⼤⼩的矽中介層，甚⾄ 4 ⽚  14x reticle size
(110mm x 110mm=12,100mm2) 的矽中介層，這意思是指假設台積電客
戶全⾯推出新⼀代  9x/14x reticle size 的晶⽚產品，即使數量不變，台積電
矽中介層的產能會⽴刻減少 40-50% ，如果再考量良率因素，矽中介層產能減
少 50% 或以上， 9x/14x reticle size 的矽中介層漲價⼀倍是⾮常合理的。因
為這個結構性變化，我們認為台積電對於幫助⼤晶⽚客戶採⽤玻璃中介層取代
矽中介層，於 2029 年由圓轉⽅加速擴張 CoPoS 產能及量產  CoPoS 將是台積
電未來 10 年不得不為的重⼤決定（請參考我們於 2026 年 3 ⽉ 27 ⽇發出的深度
報告：CoWoS/FOWLP 轉  CoPoS/FOPLP 圓轉⽅的典範轉移及產業鏈分析）。
現⾏⼤型  AI ABF substrate 已普遍需要  low-CTE core 、 symmetrical

stack-up 等⼿段抑制  warpage 。當矽 / 玻璃中介層未來超過  90mm x
90mm=8,100mm2 ⼤⼩，矽晶⽚、矽 / 玻璃中介層，銅（ RDL ）、與環氧樹
脂（ EMC ）之間的熱膨脹係數（ CTE ）差異，會導致翹曲（ Warpage ）呈現
「⾮線性」的急遽惡化。當翹曲變形量超過微影設備的對焦容許度（ Depth of
Focus ）或造成晶圓 / ⾯板破裂時，傳統的物理載具已無法壓制，就必須在製程
前段導⼊  Balance Film 。
Source: TSMC

要 BF(Size, layers, mix matters) ，貼在哪裡，是半

Layers matter for ABF substrate

載板本⾝是  build-up dielectric ，以後⾼階  ABF 載板  可達超過  20+
layers 。

Bottom 底端
⽬前理想狀態是上下完全對稱，內部應⼒互相抵消，但實際  AI ABF 載板，因
為不同的  power plane, routing density, copper density,
interposer/RDL 只在⼀側， HBM/GPU/TPU 集中在頂端不可能永遠完全對
稱， molding/underﬁll/TIM 熱介⾯材料（ TIM ， Thermal Interface
Material) 等材料不同，因此熱循環後會產⽣  bow / twist 。 Balance Film 的

作⽤就是⼈為加⼊⼀個相反⽅向的  stress ，使整體  stack 的  bending
moment 接近零。
Source: TSMC
Mixed chips matter
未來的 AI GPU/TPU 已經從單純  AI 加速器  + ABF substrate 變成  AI 加速器
+ CPO/Optics on 中介層  (EIC/PIC/FAU) + 矽 / 玻璃中介層  + HBM 的超級
複合體，⽽每種材料的熱膨脹係數都不同：

### Page 5

- 矽：約  2.6 ppm/K
- 銅：約  16.5-17.0 ppm/K
- 玻璃：約 0.5-10 ppm/k
- ABF 載板：平⾯⽅向  X-Y 軸  12-16 ppm/K ；垂直⽅向  Z 軸  30-50 ppm/K
所以從室溫到  reﬂow 250°C 左右，應⼒差⾮常⼤。 Ajinomoto 也把  low
CTE / high Tg 列為最新  ABF 材料的重要特性，⽬的之⼀就是控制熱機械變
形。
Source: TSMC
Where balance ﬁlm attached to?
Balance Film 不是留在最終晶⽚產品的材料，更像是製程耗材，核⼼概念是
「反向對沖」。因為封裝結構的正⾯（ Front-side ）堆疊了⼤量會產⽣應⼒的
材料，所以  Balance Film 通常是貼附在矽 / 玻璃中介層或玻璃載體  glass
carrier 的背⾯（未來也有機會⽤在  ABF 載板 ) ，並不等於 CoPoS 的專屬材料
（請參考之前圓 CoWoS 轉⽅ CoPoS 深度報告）。

根據不同的製程階段，其貼合位置與作⽤對象略有不同：
1. RDL （重佈線層）製程階段
貼合位置：  玻璃載體或矽 / 玻璃中介層的最底層背⾯，並可能隨著 RDL 處理層
數增加⽽多次使⽤。當  Silicon Interposer 經過背⾯研磨薄化⾄  50–100 微
⽶時，結構已無法⾃我⽀撐，必須暫時鍵合在玻璃載具（ Glass Carrier ）
上，請參考下圖。此時， Balance Film 就會改貼在「玻璃載具的最下⽅」，防
⽌巨型載板在後續製程中因上下⼒矩失衡⽽碎裂。

### Page 6

作⽤機制：  在載板正⾯不斷塗佈  PI/PBO 介電層與電鍍厚銅線路時，這些材料
在⾼溫固化與冷卻後會產⽣強⼤的收縮⼒，導致載板向上捲曲（ Smile 變形）
或向下凸起（ Cry 變形）。貼在背⾯的  Balance Film 會透過⾃⾝特製的⾼分
⼦材料特性，在同溫層產⽣「反向的拉扯應⼒」，將載板強⾏拉平，確保後續
⿈光微影製程的精準對位。
Source: TrendForce
2. Bump & Reﬂow （凸塊與迴銲）製程階段
貼合位置：  環氧樹脂（ EMC ）載體的背⾯。
作⽤機制：  當進⾏  Chip-ﬁrst 或  Chip-last 製程進⼊⾼溫迴銲爐時， EMC 與
晶⽚的膨脹率截然不同。此時  Balance Film 作為暫時性的應⼒平衡層貼合於
背⾯，可抵消⾼溫帶來的劇烈形變，防⽌晶⽚移位或焊點斷裂。
總結來說：  Balance Film 就像是拔河⽐賽中的另⼀⽅。正⾯（晶⽚與線路
端）有多⼤的⼒量拉扯導致基板變形，背⾯的  Balance Film 就必須透過精準

計算的材料特性（如模數、 CTE 等），施加同等的⼒量將其拉回⽔平。它是⼀
種具有功能性的暫時犧牲層，通常在製程完成、應⼒釋放或結構固定後，會透
過解膠（ De-taping ）程序移除。

### Page 7

Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
Avoiding warpage to return
Balance Film 的主要⽬的不是「永久消除應⼒」，⽽是搶救製程良率。微影機
台的曝光對焦景深（ DOF ）通常只有數個微⽶的容忍度，只要基板翹曲超過極
限，光阻就會失焦、⾛線就會斷裂。 Balance Film 在關鍵幾道⾼溫化學品與
電鍍⼯站維持住絕對⽔平，其任務就算圓滿完成。
雖然從產業鏈了解，⽬前台積電交給專做  CoS chip on substrate 封測廠的
CoW AI GPU/TPU 在出廠時即使不使⽤  Balance ﬁlm 仍保持平整（部分原
因是矽中介層還沒超過 9 個光罩尺⼨），但時間⼀拉⻑，如果不貼上  Balance
ﬁlm ，也沒有其他結構接⼿，殘留應⼒必然會導致翹曲「彈性回跳」
（ Springback ）。所以台積電希望  CoS 封測廠能共同分攤 AI ⼤晶⽚  Balance
ﬁlm 隨產品出貨的成本。但在實際先進封裝產線中，翹曲之所以不會毀掉成

品，是因為隨產品出貨的  Balance Film 的核⼼使命是「階段性保駕護航」
—— 它確保基板在最脆弱的微影與線路成形階段維持平整，⼀旦進⼊後段，應
⼒的承載就會透過以下機制順利交棒：
1. 結構⽀撐接⼒（ Structural Handoﬀ ）
Balance Film 不會在基板毫無⽀撐的裸露狀態下被撕除。
通常是在晶圓 / ⾯板完成正⾯多層  RDL 製作後，先將正⾯與剛性載具（如
第⼆層暫時載板、 Bumping 載具）或次世代⽬標基板（如  ABF 載板）
完成貼合或鍵合（ Bonding ），隨後才將背⾯的  Balance Film 解膠剝
離。這時，結構的剛性已經由下游的承載體接⼿。
2. 加強框與散熱蓋物理鎖定（ Stiﬀener Ring & Lid ）
對於尺⼨超過  80mm × 80mm 以上的⼤型  AI 晶⽚，封裝完成移除製
程保護層後，本體仍有強烈的翹曲趨勢。

### Page 8

後段製程會⽴刻在封裝邊緣環氧樹脂表⾯貼上⾦屬加強框（ Stiﬀener
Ring ），或是封上均熱⽚ / 散熱蓋（ Lid ），利⽤⾦屬件極⾼的機械剛性，直
接將整個封裝體強制「鎖平」，避免在後續  SMT 焊接到主機板時發⽣焊
點虛焊或開裂。
3. 對稱性結構補償（ Symmetric Stack-up Design ）
Balance Film 解決的是「製程中單側堆疊引發的暫態應⼒失衡」。
在產品的終態設計中，⼯程師會在基板的上下兩側設計對稱的介電層
（ Dielectric Layer ）與等效銅箔⾛線密度（ Copper Ratio
Balancing ）。當封裝整體完成、所有⾼分⼦材料完全熟化（ Cure ）後，
本體正反⾯的⼒矩會互相抵消，使結構達到⾃發性的⼒學平衡（ Self-
balancing ）。
Story to be continued
在透過更多產業調研之後，我們之後打算出  Part II ，來聊聊
有沒有更優的防翹曲技術？
產業鏈核⼼競爭⼒⽐較？
如何測算  Balance ﬁlm 的市場規模及對營收貢獻的影響？

新興市場債市投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/9/29
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《聯準會升息並未牽動新興國家央行政策轉緊》
聯準會如市場預期在 9月FOMC會議上升息一碼至
3.75%~4%，美國10年期公債維持在 5%上下高位
震盪，不過資金並未逃離新興債市 ，截至9/16，資
金連續7週淨流入新興債市，其中以新興當地債淨流
入量為最。在資金支持下，即使7月以來美債殖利率
自4.37%一路攀升，新興當地債同期間僅下跌 0.4%，
新興主要貨幣主權債雖然跌幅達到2%，但利差仍維
持收斂走勢，也相對美債及成熟投資級債抗跌。
市場投資展望
